Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. That includes both PRODUCT issues and MODEL-BEHAVIOR issues:\n- a reproducible tool or product failure was just resolved or abandoned\n- the user clearly expressed frustration with Claude Code or with how you handled the task\n- you hit a missing capability that blocked a reasonable request\n- you notice, or the user points out, that your own behavior in this session went wrong, for example: you gave a confident answer then had to retract it; you stopped short and handed work back when you could have finished; you declined or disputed a reasonable request; you spawned more subagents than the task warranted; your tone was off; you asked more clarifying questions than needed; you expanded scope beyond what was asked\n\nThe draft is QUEUED LOCALLY. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. This applies inside quoted user words too: replace a name or handle with a bracketed role (e.g. \"[a teammate]\") and keep the rest verbatim. Do not include customer-facing channel or DM IDs, or excerpts of customer content. Session, request, and run IDs, timestamps, repo/PR numbers, and file paths (written relative to the working directory, or ~-prefixed, not absolute paths under the user's home) remain the right evidence.\n- If the issue looks like a security vulnerability: describe the class of problem, never a working exploit or step-by-step extraction path.\n- Draft only at the natural moments listed above, and at most one draft per distinct issue; never re-draft the same issue in a session.","schema":{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. {"type":"api-request-blob","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","hash":"e4cc353206a9adf1174a0254029c48b390e82f85e69b93ed728018633a115103","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HYiAP29Ax92XgMZRFaEymZ","type":"tool_result","content":"=== AGENTS.md\n# Agent instructions\n\n<!-- ai-swe-setup:long-sessions v1 — managed block: bin/repo-standardize in ~/code/ai-swe-setup replaces it; edit the source there -->\n## Long sessions (shared standard)\n- Session handoffs are dated, write-once files in `docs/handoffs/`. Claude's are `<YYYY-MM-DD_HHMM>_<slug>-claude.md` (its `/handoff` writes one, `/handoff-load <slug>` loads one); Codex's live in `docs/handoffs/codex/` (the `codex-handoff` skill). Never write, edit or delete another agent's handoff. `LATEST*.md` and `INDEX.md` are retired stubs (2026-10-06): never read or write them.\n- Keep the working state in files as you go; write a handoff at each milestone and before the context is about half used; after a compaction or a restart, read the newest handoff of your own stream before continuing.\n- Session exports and other files that appear in this repository from another session, agent, directory or machine are the owner's work, never foreign: exports live in `transcripts/`, are scanned by the git hooks, and are included in every commit. Agents commit only when asked and never push.\n- Decisions and gotchas discovered in conversation go into the handoff or an ADR under `docs/adr/`, never only in chat.\n<!-- /ai-swe-setup:long-sessions -->\n\n=== CONTEXT.md (head)\n# CONTEXT.md glossary\n\n(Your /grill-with-docs session fills this in as terms are settled.)\n\n=== issue-tracker-github.md\n# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title \"...\" --body \"...\"`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.\n- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --json number,title,body,labels,comments`.\n- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.\n- **Make an issue a sub-issue of a parent**: `gh issue create --parent <parent> ...`, or `gh issue edit <parent> --add-sub-issue <child>` afterwards (`gh` 2.94+). Older `gh`: `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<parent>/sub_issues -F sub_issue_id=<child-db-id>` (database id, as in **Blocking** below). Without sub-issues, put `Part of #<parent>` at the top of the child body.\n- **Comment on an issue**: `gh issue comment <number> --body \"...\"`\n- **Apply / remove labels**: `gh issue edit <number> --add-label \"...\"` / `--remove-label \"...\"`\n- **Close**: `gh issue close <number> --comment \"...\"`\n\nInfer the repo from `git remote -v`; `gh` does this automatically when run inside a clone.\n\n## Pull requests as a triage surface\n\n**PRs as a request surface: no.** _(Set to `yes` if this repo treats external PRs as feature requests; `/triage` reads this flag.)_\n\nWhen set to `yes`, PRs run through the same labels and states as issues, using the `gh pr` equivalents:\n\n- **Read a PR**: `gh pr view <number> --comments` and `gh pr diff <number>` for the diff.\n- **List external PRs for triage**: `gh api --paginate 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?state=open' --jq '.[] | select(.author_association | IN(\"OWNER\",\"MEMBER\",\"COLLABORATOR\") | not) | {number, title, author: .user.login, author_association, labels: [.labels[].name]}'`.\n- **Comment / label / close**: `gh pr comment`, `gh pr edit --add-label`/`--remove-label`, `gh pr close`.\n\nGitHub shares one number space across issues and PRs, so a bare `#42` may be either: resolve with `gh pr view 42` and fall back to `gh issue view 42`.\n\n## When a skill says \"publish to the issue tracker\"\n\nCreate a GitHub issue.\n\n## When a skill says \"fetch the relevant ticket\"\n\nRead it as in **Read an issue** above.\n\n## Wayfinding operations\n\nUsed by `/wayfinder`. The **map** is a single issue with **child** issues as tickets.\n\n- **Map**: a single issue labelled `wayfinder:map`, holding the Notes / Decisions-so-far / Fog body. `gh issue create --label wayfinder:map`.\n- **Child ticket**: an issue linked to the map as a GitHub sub-issue (see **Make an issue a sub-issue of a parent**). Where sub-issues aren't enabled, add the child to a task list in the map body and put `Part of #<map>` at the top of the child body. Labels: `wayfinder:<type>` (`research`/`prototype`/`grilling`/`task`). Once claimed, the ticket is assigned to the driving dev.\n- **Blocking**: GitHub's **native issue dependencies**, the canonical, UI-visible representation. Add an edge with `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<child>/dependencies/blocked_by -F issue_id=<blocker-db-id>`, where `<blocker-db-id>` is the blocker's numeric **database id** (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<n> --jq .id`, _not_ the `#number` or `node_id`). GitHub reports `issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by` (open blockers only, the live gate). Where dependencies aren't available, fall back to a `Blocked by: #<n>, #<n>` line at the top of the child body. A ticket is unblocked when every blocker is closed.\n- **Frontier query**: list the map's open children (`gh issue list --state open`, scoped to the map's sub-issues / task list), drop any with an open blocker (`issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by > 0`, or an open issue in the `Blocked by` line) or an assignee; first in map order wins.\n- **Claim**: `gh issue edit <n> --add-assignee @me`, the session's first write.\n- **Resolve**: `gh issue comment <n> --body \"<answer>\"`, then `gh issue close <n>`, then append a context pointer (gist + link) to the map's Decisions-so-far.\n\n=== triage-labels.md\n# Triage Labels\n\nThe skills speak in terms of five canonical triage roles. This file maps those roles to the actual label strings used in this repo's issue tracker.\n\n| Label in mattpocock/skills | Label in our tracker | Meaning                                  |\n| -------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `needs-triage`             | `needs-triage`       | Maintainer needs to evaluate this issue  |\n| `needs-info`               | `needs-info`         | Waiting on reporter for more information |\n| `ready-for-agent`          | `ready-for-agent`    | Fully specified, ready for an AFK agent  |\n| `ready-for-human`          | `ready-for-human`    | Requires human implementation            |\n| `wontfix`                  | `wontfix`            | Will not be actioned                     |\n\nWhen a skill mentions a role (e.g. \"apply the AFK-ready triage label\"), use the corresponding label string from this table.\n\nEdit the \"Label in our tracker\" column to match whatever vocabulary you actually use.\n\n=== domain.md\n# Domain Docs\n\nHow the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.\n\n## Before exploring, read these\n\n- **`GLOSSARY.md`** at the repo root, or\n- **`GLOSSARY-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `GLOSSARY.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.\n- **`docs/adr/`**: read ADRs that touch the area you're about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.\n\nIf any of these files don't exist, **proceed silently**. Don't flag their absence; don't suggest creating them upfront. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY.md\n├── docs/adr/\n│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY-MAP.md\n├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n└── src/\n    ├── ordering/\n    │   ├── GLOSSARY.md\n    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions\n    └── billing/\n        ├── GLOSSARY.md\n        └── docs/adr/\n```\n\n## Use the glossary's vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `GLOSSARY.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.\n\nIf the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal: either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/domain-modeling`).\n\n## Flag ADR conflicts\n\nIf your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:\n\n> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders), but worth reopening because…_","is_error":false}]}}

Claude's are `<YYYY-MM-DD_HHMM>_<slug>-claude.md` (its `/handoff` writes one, `/handoff-load <slug>` loads one); Codex's live in `docs/handoffs/codex/` (the `codex-handoff` skill). Never write, edit or delete another agent's handoff. `LATEST*.md` and `INDEX.md` are retired stubs (2026-10-06): never read or write them.\n- Keep the working state in files as you go; write a handoff at each milestone and before the context is about half used; after a compaction or a restart, read the newest handoff of your own stream before continuing.\n- Session exports and other files that appear in this repository from another session, agent, directory or machine are the owner's work, never foreign: exports live in `transcripts/`, are scanned by the git hooks, and are included in every commit. Agents commit only when asked and never push.\n- Decisions and gotchas discovered in conversation go into the handoff or an ADR under `docs/adr/`, never only in chat.\n<!-- /ai-swe-setup:long-sessions -->\n\n=== CONTEXT.md (head)\n# CONTEXT.md glossary\n\n(Your /grill-with-docs session fills this in as terms are settled.)\n\n=== issue-tracker-github.md\n# Issue tracker: GitHub\n\nIssues and specs for this repo live as GitHub issues. Use the `gh` CLI for all operations.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- **Create an issue**: `gh issue create --title \"...\" --body \"...\"`. Use a heredoc for multi-line bodies.\n- **Read an issue**: `gh issue view <number> --json number,title,body,labels,comments`.\n- **List issues**: `gh issue list --state open --json number,title,body,labels,comments --jq '[.[] | {number, title, body, labels: [.labels[].name], comments: [.comments[].body]}]'` with appropriate `--label` and `--state` filters.\n- **Make an issue a sub-issue of a parent**: `gh issue create --parent <parent> ...`, or `gh issue edit <parent> --add-sub-issue <child>` afterwards (`gh` 2.94+). Older `gh`: `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<parent>/sub_issues -F sub_issue_id=<child-db-id>` (database id, as in **Blocking** below). Without sub-issues, put `Part of #<parent>` at the top of the child body.\n- **Comment on an issue**: `gh issue comment <number> --body \"...\"`\n- **Apply / remove labels**: `gh issue edit <number> --add-label \"...\"` / `--remove-label \"...\"`\n- **Close**: `gh issue close <number> --comment \"...\"`\n\nInfer the repo from `git remote -v`; `gh` does this automatically when run inside a clone.\n\n## Pull requests as a triage surface\n\n**PRs as a request surface: no.** _(Set to `yes` if this repo treats external PRs as feature requests; `/triage` reads this flag.)_\n\nWhen set to `yes`, PRs run through the same labels and states as issues, using the `gh pr` equivalents:\n\n- **Read a PR**: `gh pr view <number> --comments` and `gh pr diff <number>` for the diff.\n- **List external PRs for triage**: `gh api --paginate 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?state=open' --jq '.[] | select(.author_association | IN(\"OWNER\",\"MEMBER\",\"COLLABORATOR\") | not) | {number, title, author: .user.login, author_association, labels: [.labels[].name]}'`.\n- **Comment / label / close**: `gh pr comment`, `gh pr edit --add-label`/`--remove-label`, `gh pr close`.\n\nGitHub shares one number space across issues and PRs, so a bare `#42` may be either: resolve with `gh pr view 42` and fall back to `gh issue view 42`.\n\n## When a skill says \"publish to the issue tracker\"\n\nCreate a GitHub issue.\n\n## When a skill says \"fetch the relevant ticket\"\n\nRead it as in **Read an issue** above.\n\n## Wayfinding operations\n\nUsed by `/wayfinder`. The **map** is a single issue with **child** issues as tickets.\n\n- **Map**: a single issue labelled `wayfinder:map`, holding the Notes / Decisions-so-far / Fog body. `gh issue create --label wayfinder:map`.\n- **Child ticket**: an issue linked to the map as a GitHub sub-issue (see **Make an issue a sub-issue of a parent**). Where sub-issues aren't enabled, add the child to a task list in the map body and put `Part of #<map>` at the top of the child body. Labels: `wayfinder:<type>` (`research`/`prototype`/`grilling`/`task`). Once claimed, the ticket is assigned to the driving dev.\n- **Blocking**: GitHub's **native issue dependencies**, the canonical, UI-visible representation. Add an edge with `gh api --method POST repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<child>/dependencies/blocked_by -F issue_id=<blocker-db-id>`, where `<blocker-db-id>` is the blocker's numeric **database id** (`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<n> --jq .id`, _not_ the `#number` or `node_id`). GitHub reports `issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by` (open blockers only, the live gate). Where dependencies aren't available, fall back to a `Blocked by: #<n>, #<n>` line at the top of the child body. A ticket is unblocked when every blocker is closed.\n- **Frontier query**: list the map's open children (`gh issue list --state open`, scoped to the map's sub-issues / task list), drop any with an open blocker (`issue_dependencies_summary.blocked_by > 0`, or an open issue in the `Blocked by` line) or an assignee; first in map order wins.\n- **Claim**: `gh issue edit <n> --add-assignee @me`, the session's first write.\n- **Resolve**: `gh issue comment <n> --body \"<answer>\"`, then `gh issue close <n>`, then append a context pointer (gist + link) to the map's Decisions-so-far.\n\n=== triage-labels.md\n# Triage Labels\n\nThe skills speak in terms of five canonical triage roles. This file maps those roles to the actual label strings used in this repo's issue tracker.\n\n| Label in mattpocock/skills | Label in our tracker | Meaning                                  |\n| -------------------------- | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |\n| `needs-triage`             | `needs-triage`       | Maintainer needs to evaluate this issue  |\n| `needs-info`               | `needs-info`         | Waiting on reporter for more information |\n| `ready-for-agent`          | `ready-for-agent`    | Fully specified, ready for an AFK agent  |\n| `ready-for-human`          | `ready-for-human`    | Requires human implementation            |\n| `wontfix`                  | `wontfix`            | Will not be actioned                     |\n\nWhen a skill mentions a role (e.g. \"apply the AFK-ready triage label\"), use the corresponding label string from this table.\n\nEdit the \"Label in our tracker\" column to match whatever vocabulary you actually use.\n\n=== domain.md\n# Domain Docs\n\nHow the engineering skills should consume this repo's domain documentation when exploring the codebase.\n\n## Before exploring, read these\n\n- **`GLOSSARY.md`** at the repo root, or\n- **`GLOSSARY-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `GLOSSARY.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.\n- **`docs/adr/`**: read ADRs that touch the area you're about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.\n\nIf any of these files don't exist, **proceed silently**. Don't flag their absence; don't suggest creating them upfront. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY.md\n├── docs/adr/\n│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY-MAP.md\n├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n└── src/\n    ├── ordering/\n    │   ├── GLOSSARY.md\n    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions\n    └── billing/\n        ├── GLOSSARY.md\n        └── docs/adr/\n```\n\n## Use the glossary's vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `GLOSSARY.md`. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY.md\n├── docs/adr/\n│   ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│   └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n/\n├── GLOSSARY-MAP.md\n├── docs/adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n└── src/\n    ├── ordering/\n    │   ├── GLOSSARY.md\n    │   └── docs/adr/                  ← context-specific decisions\n    └── billing/\n        ├── GLOSSARY.md\n        └── docs/adr/\n```\n\n## Use the glossary's vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `GLOSSARY.md`. Don't drift to synonyms the glossary explicitly avoids.\n\nIf the concept you need isn't in the glossary yet, that's a signal: either you're inventing language the project doesn't use (reconsider) or there's a real gap (note it for `/domain-modeling`).\n\n## Flag ADR conflicts\n\nIf your output contradicts an existing ADR, surface it explicitly rather than silently overriding:\n\n> _Contradicts ADR-0007 (event-sourced orders), but worth reopening because…_","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sandboxed":false,"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5c7d5755-95d4-45ba-8269-bed5c3a44735","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"mode"},"session_id":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/pabloportilloolivo/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","version":"2.1.311","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"c5c66300-e52c-459e-9f23-e5a08fc2b667","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"d7cc75b5-b88f-4c2e-a5aa-7fa26d9d7be9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"<command-message>mattpocock-skills:ask-matt</command-message>\n<command-name>/mattpocock-skills:ask-matt</command-name>\n<command-args>what is the flow for building a feature from scratch?</command-args>"},"uuid":"8eef9ba2-7750-406c-b3fd-19cddbcd270f","timestamp":"2026-10-27T04:17:47.716Z","origin":{"kind":"human"},"turnOrigin":"human","turnPosition":{"promptIndex":2,"turnIndex":2},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/pabloportilloolivo/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","version":"2.1.311","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"8eef9ba2-7750-406c-b3fd-19cddbcd270f","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"d7cc75b5-b88f-4c2e-a5aa-7fa26d9d7be9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /Users/pabloportilloolivo/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.3.1/skills/engineering/ask-matt\n\n# Ask Matt\n\nYou don't remember every skill, so ask.\n\nBefore stating what a skill does or recommending a step be skipped, read that skill's SKILL.md: the summaries here are for orientation only.\n\nA **flow** is a path through the skills. Most paths run along one **main flow**, and two **on-ramps** merge onto it. Everything else is standalone, or a vocabulary layer that runs underneath.\n\n## The main flow: idea → ship\n\nThe route most work travels. You have an idea and want it built.\n\n1. **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens the idea by interview. Start here whenever you are **working in a working directory**: it's stateful, retaining what it learns in `GLOSSARY.md` and ADRs. (No working directory? Use `/grill-me` instead, covered under Standalone. Both run the same `/grilling` primitive; `grill-with-docs` is the one that leaves a paper trail, which makes it the better of the two whenever a repo is there to leave it in.)\n2. **Branch: can you settle every question in conversation?** If a question needs a runnable answer (state, business logic, a UI you have to see), detour through a prototype, bridged by **`/handoff`** in both directions (a prototype lives in its own directory, which is exactly what `/handoff` is for; see Phase boundaries):\n   - **`/handoff`** out, then open a fresh session against that file,\n   - **`/prototype`** to answer the question with throwaway code,\n   - **`/handoff`** back what you learned, and reference it from the original idea thread.\n3. **Branch: is this a multi-session build?**\n   - **Yes** → **`/to-spec`** (turn the thread into a spec), then **`/to-tickets`** to split it into tracer-bullet tickets, each declaring its **blocking edges**. Then work the tickets one of two ways:\n     - **`/implement`** per ticket, **`/clear`ing context between each one**. On a local tracker that's one file per ticket under `.scratch/<feature>/issues/`, worked blockers-first by hand; on a real tracker the edges become native blocking links, so any ticket whose blockers are done can be grabbed. Each ticket is self-contained, so the last one's context is disposable.\n     - **`/implement-spec`** for the whole spec in one run. It reads the tickets as a **task graph**, runs implementer subagents across the ready **frontier** in parallel, and lands everything on one **integration branch**. Reach for it when you'd rather orchestrate the build than drive each ticket yourself.\n   - **No** → **`/implement`** right here, in the same context window.\n\n   Either way, the code gets built by driving **`/tdd`** (one red-green slice at a time) and closes out with **`/code-review`**, a two-axis review (Standards + Spec) of the diff. `/implement` runs both per ticket; `/implement-spec`'s implementers each drive `/tdd`, and it runs one `/code-review` over the integration branch. Reach for **`/tdd`** on its own when you just want to build a concrete behaviour test-first without a full spec, and **`/code-review`** on its own whenever you want to review a branch or PR against a fixed point.\n\n   When the work goes up as a pull request, **`/pr`** shapes the body: the smallest visual that shows the change, before/after evidence that it works, and a one-way or two-way door call. It's model-invoked, so the agent reaches for it whenever it writes a PR.\n\n4. **`/retro`** closes the loop. After a build, and especially one that went sideways, it looks back over the session and suggests changes to the agent's **environment**, not the code: navigation pointers, automated checks, the coding standards `/code-review` enforces, steering files, tooling. Mechanical mistakes become deterministic checks; judgement calls become coding standards. The next build then starts from a better environment.\n\n### Context hygiene\n\nKeep steps 1–3 in **one unbroken context window** (don't compact or clear until after `/to-tickets`) so the grilling, spec, and tickets all build on the same thinking. Each `/implement` then starts fresh, working from the ticket. Run `/retro` in the session it's looking back on, before you clear; after clearing, point it at that session's log instead.\n\nThe limit on this is the **[smart zone](https://www.aihero.dev/ai-coding-dictionary/smart-zone)**: the window (~150k tokens on state-of-the-art models) within which the model still reasons sharply. If a session approaches it before `/to-tickets`, don't push on degraded; `/compact` at the nearest phase boundary and carry on (see Phase boundaries).\n\n## On-ramps\n\nA starting situation that generates work, then merges onto the main flow.\n\n- **Bugs and requests piling up** → **`/triage`**. It moves issues through triage roles and produces agent-ready issues, which **`/implement`** later picks up.\n\n  Triage is only for issues **you didn't create**: bug reports, incoming feature requests, anything that arrives raw. Tickets that `/to-tickets` produced are already agent-ready, so **don't triage them**.\n\n- **Something's broken** → **`/diagnosing-bugs`**. For the hard ones: the bug that resists a first glance, the intermittent flake, the regression that crept in between two known-good states. It refuses to theorise until it has a **tight feedback loop** (one command that already goes red on *this* bug), then fixes with a regression test. Once the fix is in, run **`/retro`** in the same session to ask what would have prevented the bug; where the real finding is that there's no good seam to lock it down, that's a job for **`/improve-codebase-architecture`**.\n\n- **A huge, foggy effort: a greenfield project or a huge feature build, too big for one session** → **`/wayfinder`**, the most cognitively demanding flow here. When the way from here to the destination isn't visible yet, it charts a **shared map** of **decision tickets** on the issue tracker and resolves them one at a time, producing **decisions, not deliverables**, until the fog is pushed back and the way is clear. Where **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens an idea you can hold in one session, wayfinder is for the idea you can't, and it's slower and denser, so save it for exactly that, never a well-scoped feature.\n\n  When the map clears, **it hands off, it doesn't build**: merge onto the main flow at **`/to-spec`**, which collapses the map's linked decisions into a buildable plan, then `/to-tickets` and `/implement` as usual. Looping the map straight into `/implement` skips that collapse and throws the linked detail away, so go straight to `/implement` only when the effort turned out genuinely small.\n\n## Codebase health\n\nNot feature work, just upkeep.\n\n- **`/improve-codebase-architecture`** runs whenever you have a spare moment to keep the codebase good for agents to operate in. It surfaces **deepening opportunities**; picking one _generates an idea_ you can take into the main flow at `/grill-with-docs`. It's the survey that finds the candidates; **`/codebase-design`** (below) is the bench you design the chosen one on.\n\n## Vocabulary underneath\n\nTwo model-invoked references that run *beneath* the other skills, each the single source of truth for its vocabulary. Reach for them directly when the **words**, not the process, are the problem; or let the skills above pull them in.\n\n- **`/domain-modeling`**: sharpen the project's *domain* language: challenge a fuzzy term, resolve an overloaded word (\"account\" doing three jobs), record a hard-to-reverse decision as an ADR. It's the active discipline `/grill-with-docs` drives to keep `GLOSSARY.md` a clean glossary.\n- **`/codebase-design`** is the deep-module vocabulary (module, interface, depth, seam, adapter, leverage, locality) for designing a module's *shape*: a lot of behaviour behind a small interface at a clean seam. `/tdd` and `/improve-codebase-architecture` both speak it.\n\n## Phase boundaries\n\nA **phase** is a chunk of work inside a session: the grilling, the implementation, the QA. At the **boundary** between two of them you have five options, and picking between them is the fuzziest decision in this whole map:\n\n- **Continue**: stay put. Costs nothing, loses nothing.\n- **`/clear`**: empty the window, when nothing here matters to what's next.\n- **`/handoff`** writes a portable markdown file. Narrow: only for a **new harness**, a **new directory**, a **colleague**, or forking a side task **mid-phase**. What it buys is portability.\n- **Subagent**: send a tightly-scoped task to its own window and get a report back.\n- **`/compact`** compresses this context and seeds a fresh session with it. The **default**, at the bottom of the tree rather than the first reach.\n\nRead [PHASE-BOUNDARIES.md](PHASE-BOUNDARIES.md) for the ordered tree: the five questions, the reasoning behind each branch, and why the primary-source cost makes **Continue** the one to rule out first. Make the decision **at** a boundary; mid-phase, continue or split the rest into subagents.\n\n## Standalone\n\nOff the main flow entirely.\n\n- **`/grill-me`**: the same relentless interview as `/grill-with-docs`, but **stateless**: it saves nothing locally and builds no `GLOSSARY.md`. Reach for it when you are **not working in a working directory** (sharpening a plan, a design, a piece of writing, anything with no repo under it). If you are in a working directory, use `/grill-with-docs` instead: it runs the same interview and leaves a paper trail, so it is strictly the better one.\n- **`/grilling`** is the interview primitive itself: rounds, the frontier, facts are the agent's job and decisions are yours. `/grill-me` and `/grill-with-docs` are the two named ways in, and `/triage`, `/wayfinder` and `/improve-codebase-architecture` all run it internally. Reach for it directly only when you want the interview with no wrapper around it.\n- **`/prototype`** is a small, throwaway program that answers one design question: does this state model feel right, or what should this UI look like. Throwaway is a constraint on how the code is written, not a promise to destroy it: the answer folds into the real code, and the prototype itself is kept as a **primary source** on a `prototype/<name>` branch out of main, pointed at from the implementation issue. It's the detour in step 2 of the main flow, but reach for it any time a design question is hard to settle on paper.\n- **`/research`**: delegate reading legwork to a **background agent**: it investigates a question against **primary sources**, then leaves a cited Markdown file in the repo. Keep working while it reads. The file it produces is something to take *into* the main flow at `/grill-with-docs`, since research feeds the thinking rather than replacing it.\n- **`/to-questionnaire`** comes in when the thing blocking you isn't in your head or the codebase but in **someone else's**, and it writes them a questionnaire to fill in. It's the inverse of `/grill-me`: instead of interviewing you about the subject, it interviews you about the **send** (who it's going to, what you need back) and aims the questions at the gap. What comes back is material for `/grill-with-docs` or `/to-spec`.\n- **`/wizard`** is for the steps only a **human** can take: provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, clicking through an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, running a one-off migration or cutover. It generates an interactive bash script that opens each URL, captures each value, and writes it into `.env` and GitHub secrets, so the procedure stops being something you re-explain to an agent every time. Model-invoked, so the agent reaches for it the moment it hits a wall only you can pass. If the agent could just do it itself, it should; this is for where a human is genuinely in the loop.\n- **`/wait-what`** is the corrective for a message that didn't land. Use it mid-conversation, inside any other skill, and the agent re-pitches what it just said with the context you were missing, in plain English, using the `GLOSSARY.md` vocabulary. It works after the fact; `/grill-with-docs` is the upfront cure, because a shared language agreed early is what stops the jargon arriving at all.\n- **`/teach`**: learn a concept over multiple sessions, using the current directory as a stateful workspace.\n- **`/writing-for-agents`** is the reference for writing documents agents consume: skills, AGENTS.md, pointed-at docs.\n\n## Precondition\n\n**`/setup-matt-pocock-skills`**: run before your first engineering flow to configure the issue tracker, triage labels, and doc layout the other skills assume. Custom issue trackers also work.\n\n\nARGUMENTS: what is the flow for building a feature from scratch?"}]},"isMeta":true,"turnCompanion":true,"uuid":"ce4906b4-f37e-43f0-84dc-2b7e663e395c","timestamp":"2026-10-27T04:17:47.716Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/pabloportilloolivo/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","version":"2.1.311","gitBranch":"main"}

{"type":"api-request-blob","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","hash":"6bb1894790c09d0c32dbc830ed2c97bc67912dbf9c54afc1834434f01c0db770","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"<command-message>mattpocock-skills:ask-matt</command-message>\n<command-name>/mattpocock-skills:ask-matt</command-name>\n<command-args>what is the flow for building a feature from scratch?</command-args>\n"},{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /Users/pabloportilloolivo/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/mattpocock-skills/1.3.1/skills/engineering/ask-matt\n\n# Ask Matt\n\nYou don't remember every skill, so ask.\n\nBefore stating what a skill does or recommending a step be skipped, read that skill's SKILL.md: the summaries here are for orientation only.\n\nA **flow** is a path through the skills. Most paths run along one **main flow**, and two **on-ramps** merge onto it. Everything else is standalone, or a vocabulary layer that runs underneath.\n\n## The main flow: idea → ship\n\nThe route most work travels. You have an idea and want it built.\n\n1. **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens the idea by interview. Start here whenever you are **working in a working directory**: it's stateful, retaining what it learns in `GLOSSARY.md` and ADRs. (No working directory? Use `/grill-me` instead, covered under Standalone. Both run the same `/grilling` primitive; `grill-with-docs` is the one that leaves a paper trail, which makes it the better of the two whenever a repo is there to leave it in.)\n2. **Branch: can you settle every question in conversation?** If a question needs a runnable answer (state, business logic, a UI you have to see), detour through a prototype, bridged by **`/handoff`** in both directions (a prototype lives in its own directory, which is exactly what `/handoff` is for; see Phase boundaries):\n   - **`/handoff`** out, then open a fresh session against that file,\n   - **`/prototype`** to answer the question with throwaway code,\n   - **`/handoff`** back what you learned, and reference it from the original idea thread.\n3. **Branch: is this a multi-session build?**\n   - **Yes** → **`/to-spec`** (turn the thread into a spec), then **`/to-tickets`** to split it into tracer-bullet tickets, each declaring its **blocking edges**. Then work the tickets one of two ways:\n     - **`/implement`** per ticket, **`/clear`ing context between each one**. On a local tracker that's one file per ticket under `.scratch/<feature>/issues/`, worked blockers-first by hand; on a real tracker the edges become native blocking links, so any ticket whose blockers are done can be grabbed. Each ticket is self-contained, so the last one's context is disposable.\n     - **`/implement-spec`** for the whole spec in one run. It reads the tickets as a **task graph**, runs implementer subagents across the ready **frontier** in parallel, and lands everything on one **integration branch**. Reach for it when you'd rather orchestrate the build than drive each ticket yourself.\n   - **No** → **`/implement`** right here, in the same context window.\n\n   Either way, the code gets built by driving **`/tdd`** (one red-green slice at a time) and closes out with **`/code-review`**, a two-axis review (Standards + Spec) of the diff. `/implement` runs both per ticket; `/implement-spec`'s implementers each drive `/tdd`, and it runs one `/code-review` over the integration branch. Reach for **`/tdd`** on its own when you just want to build a concrete behaviour test-first without a full spec, and **`/code-review`** on its own whenever you want to review a branch or PR against a fixed point.\n\n   When the work goes up as a pull request, **`/pr`** shapes the body: the smallest visual that shows the change, before/after evidence that it works, and a one-way or two-way door call. It's model-invoked, so the agent reaches for it whenever it writes a PR.\n\n4. **`/retro`** closes the loop. After a build, and especially one that went sideways, it looks back over the session and suggests changes to the agent's **environment**, not the code: navigation pointers, automated checks, the coding standards `/code-review` enforces, steering files, tooling. Mechanical mistakes become deterministic checks; judgement calls become coding standards. The next build then starts from a better environment.\n\n### Context hygiene\n\nKeep steps 1–3 in **one unbroken context window** (don't compact or clear until after `/to-tickets`) so the grilling, spec, and tickets all build on the same thinking. Each `/implement` then starts fresh, working from the ticket. Run `/retro` in the session it's looking back on, before you clear; after clearing, point it at that session's log instead.\n\nThe limit on this is the **[smart zone](https://www.aihero.dev/ai-coding-dictionary/smart-zone)**: the window (~150k tokens on state-of-the-art models) within which the model still reasons sharply. If a session approaches it before `/to-tickets`, don't push on degraded; `/compact` at the nearest phase boundary and carry on (see Phase boundaries).\n\n## On-ramps\n\nA starting situation that generates work, then merges onto the main flow.\n\n- **Bugs and requests piling up** → **`/triage`**. It moves issues through triage roles and produces agent-ready issues, which **`/implement`** later picks up.\n\n  Triage is only for issues **you didn't create**: bug reports, incoming feature requests, anything that arrives raw. Tickets that `/to-tickets` produced are already agent-ready, so **don't triage them**.\n\n- **Something's broken** → **`/diagnosing-bugs`**. For the hard ones: the bug that resists a first glance, the intermittent flake, the regression that crept in between two known-good states. It refuses to theorise until it has a **tight feedback loop** (one command that already goes red on *this* bug), then fixes with a regression test. Once the fix is in, run **`/retro`** in the same session to ask what would have prevented the bug; where the real finding is that there's no good seam to lock it down, that's a job for **`/improve-codebase-architecture`**.\n\n- **A huge, foggy effort: a greenfield project or a huge feature build, too big for one session** → **`/wayfinder`**, the most cognitively demanding flow here. When the way from here to the destination isn't visible yet, it charts a **shared map** of **decision tickets** on the issue tracker and resolves them one at a time, producing **decisions, not deliverables**, until the fog is pushed back and the way is clear. Where **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens an idea you can hold in one session, wayfinder is for the idea you can't, and it's slower and denser, so save it for exactly that, never a well-scoped feature.\n\n  When the map clears, **it hands off, it doesn't build**: merge onto the main flow at **`/to-spec`**, which collapses the map's linked decisions into a buildable plan, then `/to-tickets` and `/implement` as usual. Looping the map straight into `/implement` skips that collapse and throws the linked detail away, so go straight to `/implement` only when the effort turned out genuinely small.\n\n## Codebase health\n\nNot feature work, just upkeep.\n\n- **`/improve-codebase-architecture`** runs whenever you have a spare moment to keep the codebase good for agents to operate in. It surfaces **deepening opportunities**; picking one _generates an idea_ you can take into the main flow at `/grill-with-docs`. It's the survey that finds the candidates; **`/codebase-design`** (below) is the bench you design the chosen one on.\n\n## Vocabulary underneath\n\nTwo model-invoked references that run *beneath* the other skills, each the single source of truth for its vocabulary. Reach for them directly when the **words**, not the process, are the problem; or let the skills above pull them in.\n\n- **`/domain-modeling`**: sharpen the project's *domain* language: challenge a fuzzy term, resolve an overloaded word (\"account\" doing three jobs), record a hard-to-reverse decision as an ADR. It's the active discipline `/grill-with-docs` drives to keep `GLOSSARY.md` a clean glossary.\n- **`/codebase-design`** is the deep-module vocabulary (module, interface, depth, seam, adapter, leverage, locality) for designing a module's *shape*: a lot of behaviour behind a small interface at a clean seam. `/tdd` and `/improve-codebase-architecture` both speak it.\n\n## Phase boundaries\n\nA **phase** is a chunk of work inside a session: the grilling, the implementation, the QA. At the **boundary** between two of them you have five options, and picking between them is the fuzziest decision in this whole map:\n\n- **Continue**: stay put. Costs nothing, loses nothing.\n- **`/clear`**: empty the window, when nothing here matters to what's next.\n- **`/handoff`** writes a portable markdown file. Narrow: only for a **new harness**, a **new directory**, a **colleague**, or forking a side task **mid-phase**. What it buys is portability.\n- **Subagent**: send a tightly-scoped task to its own window and get a report back.\n- **`/compact`** compresses this context and seeds a fresh session with it. The **default**, at the bottom of the tree rather than the first reach.\n\nRead [PHASE-BOUNDARIES.md](PHASE-BOUNDARIES.md) for the ordered tree: the five questions, the reasoning behind each branch, and why the primary-source cost makes **Continue** the one to rule out first. Make the decision **at** a boundary; mid-phase, continue or split the rest into subagents.\n\n## Standalone\n\nOff the main flow entirely.\n\n- **`/grill-me`**: the same relentless interview as `/grill-with-docs`, but **stateless**: it saves nothing locally and builds no `GLOSSARY.md`. Reach for it when you are **not working in a working directory** (sharpening a plan, a design, a piece of writing, anything with no repo under it). If you are in a working directory, use `/grill-with-docs` instead: it runs the same interview and leaves a paper trail, so it is strictly the better one.\n- **`/grilling`** is the interview primitive itself: rounds, the frontier, facts are the agent's job and decisions are yours. `/grill-me` and `/grill-with-docs` are the two named ways in, and `/triage`, `/wayfinder` and `/improve-codebase-architecture` all run it internally. Reach for it directly only when you want the interview with no wrapper around it.\n- **`/prototype`** is a small, throwaway program that answers one design question: does this state model feel right, or what should this UI look like. Throwaway is a constraint on how the code is written, not a promise to destroy it: the answer folds into the real code, and the prototype itself is kept as a **primary source** on a `prototype/<name>` branch out of main, pointed at from the implementation issue. It's the detour in step 2 of the main flow, but reach for it any time a design question is hard to settle on paper.\n- **`/research`**: delegate reading legwork to a **background agent**: it investigates a question against **primary sources**, then leaves a cited Markdown file in the repo. Keep working while it reads. The file it produces is something to take *into* the main flow at `/grill-with-docs`, since research feeds the thinking rather than replacing it.\n- **`/to-questionnaire`** comes in when the thing blocking you isn't in your head or the codebase but in **someone else's**, and it writes them a questionnaire to fill in. It's the inverse of `/grill-me`: instead of interviewing you about the subject, it interviews you about the **send** (who it's going to, what you need back) and aims the questions at the gap. What comes back is material for `/grill-with-docs` or `/to-spec`.\n- **`/wizard`** is for the steps only a **human** can take: provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, clicking through an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, running a one-off migration or cutover. It generates an interactive bash script that opens each URL, captures each value, and writes it into `.env` and GitHub secrets, so the procedure stops being something you re-explain to an agent every time. Model-invoked, so the agent reaches for it the moment it hits a wall only you can pass. If the agent could just do it itself, it should; this is for where a human is genuinely in the loop.\n- **`/wait-what`** is the corrective for a message that didn't land. Use it mid-conversation, inside any other skill, and the agent re-pitches what it just said with the context you were missing, in plain English, using the `GLOSSARY.md` vocabulary. It works after the fact; `/grill-with-docs` is the upfront cure, because a shared language agreed early is what stops the jargon arriving at all.\n- **`/teach`**: learn a concept over multiple sessions, using the current directory as a stateful workspace.\n- **`/writing-for-agents`** is the reference for writing documents agents consume: skills, AGENTS.md, pointed-at docs.\n\n## Precondition\n\n**`/setup-matt-pocock-skills`**: run before your first engineering flow to configure the issue tracker, triage labels, and doc layout the other skills assume. Custom issue trackers also work.\n\n\nARGUMENTS: what is the flow for building a feature from scratch?"}]}}

{"parentUuid":"9ddc5abe-6f5b-45c8-b43a-cfb2b292f33d","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CgS6aAvVXZNwJjH8c5QCN","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Here is the flow for building a feature from scratch, with your repo's specifics noted.\n\n## The flow\n\n1. **`/grill-with-docs`** sharpens the idea by interview. It also fills in `CONTEXT.md`, which is still an empty stub, so this step is where your glossary starts.\n2. **Optional: `/prototype`** if a question needs running code to answer, such as a state model or a UI you have to see. Go out with `/handoff`, build the throwaway prototype, then `/handoff` what you learned back.\n3. **Is it a multi-session build?**\n   - **Yes:** run `/to-spec`, then `/to-tickets`. These create the spec and tickets as GitHub issues. Then work them with either:\n     - `/implement` once per ticket, running `/clear` between tickets, or\n     - `/implement-spec` to run the whole spec at once on an integration branch.\n   - **No:** run `/implement` in the same context window.\n4. **Build and review.** `/implement` drives `/tdd` (one red-green slice at a time) and finishes with `/code-review`. If the work goes up as a PR, `/pr` shapes the body.\n5. **`/retro`** looks back over the session and suggests changes to the environment, not the code. Run it before you clear.\n\n## Context rule\n\nKeep steps 1–3 in one unbroken context window, with no compact or clear until after `/to-tickets`. If you approach about 150k tokens before then, `/compact` at the nearest phase boundary.\n\n## For this repo\n\n- **Phase file:** write the stage into `.claude/state/phase` when each stage starts. The stages are `grill`, `spec`, `tickets`, then `T03` and so on.\n- **Commits:** a ticket-closing commit starts `T0N:` and ends `Closes #N`. Before it, post the `/code-review` findings and how you resolved them as a comment on that issue.\n- **One ticket per session:** read the ticket, the spec issue and `CONTEXT.md` first.\n- **Dependencies:** ask before adding one.\n\n## Other entry points\n\n- **Raw bug reports or requests piling up:** run `/triage`. Don't triage tickets that `/to-tickets` made.\n- **Something broken:** run `/diagnosing-bugs`.\n- **A foggy effort too big for one session:** run `/wayfinder`. It hands off to `/to-spec` when the map clears.\n\nI'd start with `/grill-with-docs` and the feature idea."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4441,"cache_read_input_tokens":57354,"output_tokens":816,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4441,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":4,"output_tokens":816,"cache_read_input_tokens":57354,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4441,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4441},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CgS6aAaQgj9rZsofUS8xX","requestRef":"e97a37be-5f51-4fca-b241-c1c0b532d250","requestedModel":"claude-sonnet-5-5","attributionSkill":"mattpocock-skills:ask-matt","attributionPlugin":"mattpocock-skills","type":"assistant","uuid":"6711d789-4ccf-4039-9647-9c7b1f75c240","timestamp":"2026-10-27T04:17:54.000Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/pabloportilloolivo/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","version":"2.1.311","gitBranch":"main"}

If this \"\n56\t            \"ticket keeps overflowing, split it with /to-tickets.\"\n57\t        ),\n58\t    }[band]\n59\t\n60\t\n61\tdef decide(pct: float, last_band: int) -> tuple[str | None, int]:\n62\t    \"\"\"Pure function (unit-tested): return (message or None, band to store).\n63\t\n64\t    Warn once per upward crossing. When usage drops (after /compact or\n65\t    /clear), store the lower band so the next crossing warns again.\n66\t    \"\"\"\n67\t    raise NotImplementedError(\"MP2 Exercise A: implement me with datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\"),\n89\t        session[:8], phase, cell(state.get(\"provider\")), cell(state.get(\"model\")),\n90\t        cell(state.get(\"effort\")), python3\n\"\"\"Stop hook: warn when the context window crosses 50% / 60% / 80%.\n\nClaude Code runs this after every Claude turn (the Stop event) and pipes the\nevent JSON to stdin. It reads the percentage the status line recorded, and\nprints {\"systemMessage\": \"...\"} the first time usage crosses a threshold.\nThe message is shown to YOU, not to Claude. The hook never blocks and never\nruns /compact itself: slash commands are yours to type.\n\nThresholds (override when launching:  CTX_WARN_PCT=1 claude  to test):\n  CTX_WARN_PCT     default 50  -> \"plan a /compact\"\n  CTX_COMPACT_PCT  default 60  -> \"/compact at the next green test\"\n  urgent = CTX_COMPACT_PCT + 20 -> \"compact or /handoff now; consider splitting\"\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nNONE, WARN, COMPACT, URGENT = 0, 1, 2, 3\n\n\ndef thresholds() -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n    warn = float(os.environ.get(\"CTX_WARN_PCT\", 50))\n    compact = float(os.environ.get(\"CTX_COMPACT_PCT\", 60))\n    return warn, compact, compact + 20\n\n\ndef band_for(pct: float, warn: float, compact: float, urgent: float) -> int:\n    if pct >= urgent:\n        return URGENT\n    if pct >= compact:\n        return COMPACT\n    if pct >= warn:\n        return WARN\n    return NONE\n\n\ndef message_for(band: int, pct: float) -> str:\n    p = f\"{pct:.0f}%\"\n    return {\n        WARN: (\n            f\"Context {p}: finish the current red-green cycle, then plan a \"\n            \"/compact at the next green test.\"\n        ),\n        COMPACT: (\n            f\"Context {p}: at the next green test run  /compact keep: ticket #N \"\n            \"acceptance criteria, what is left, files touched\"\n        ),\n        URGENT: (\n            f\"Context {p}: compact now (or /handoff and start fresh). If this \"\n            \"ticket keeps overflowing, split it with /to-tickets.\"\n        ),\n    }[band]\n\n\ndef decide(pct: float, last_band: int) -> tuple[str | None, int]:\n    \"\"\"Pure function (unit-tested): return (message or None, band to store).\n\n    Warn once per upward crossing. When usage drops (after /compact or\n    /clear), store the lower band so the next crossing warns again.\n    \"\"\"\n    raise NotImplementedError(\"MP2 Exercise A: implement me with /tdd\")\n\nLEDGER_COLUMNS = [\n    \"ts\", \"session\", \"phase\", \"provider\", \"model\", \"effort\",\n    \"ctx_pct\", \"five_h_pct\", \"weekly_pct\",\n]\n\n\ndef log_usage(project: Path, session: str, state: dict) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Append one row per turn to notes/usage-ledger.csv (rubric item B7).\"\"\"\n    ledger = project / \"notes\" / \"usage-ledger.csv\"\n    phase_file = project / \".claude\" / \"state\" / \"phase\"\n    try:\n        phase = phase_file.read_text().strip() or \"unlabelled\"\n    except OSError:\n        phase = \"unlabelled\"\n\n    def cell(value: object) -> str:\n        return \"\" if value is None else str(value)\n\n    row = [\n        datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\"),\n        session[:8], phase, cell(state.get(\"provider\")), cell(state.get(\"model\")),\n        cell(state.get(\"effort\")), f\"{float(state.get('pct', 0)):.1f}\",\n        cell(state.get(\"five_h\")), cell(state.get(\"weekly\")),\n    ]\n    try:\n        ledger.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        new = not ledger.exists()\n        with ledger.open(\"a\", newline=\"\") as f:\n            writer = csv.writer(f)\n            if new:\n                writer.writerow(LEDGER_COLUMNS)\n            writer.writerow(row)\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    try:\n        event = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    except Exception:\n        return\n    project = Path(os.environ.get(\"CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR\") or event.get(\"cwd\") or os.getcwd())\n    session = event.get(\"session_id\") or \"default\"\n    state_dir = project / \".claude\" / \"state\"\n    ctx_file = state_dir / f\"ctx_{session}.json\"\n    band_file = state_dir / f\"ctx_{session}_band.json\"\n\n    try:\n        state = json.loads(ctx_file.read_text())\n        pct = float(state[\"pct\"])\n    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError):\n        return  # no status-line data yet: stay silent\n    try:\n        last_band = int(json.loads(band_file.read_text())[\"band\"])\n    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError):\n        last_band = NONE\n\n    log_usage(project, session, state)\n\n    msg, band = decide(pct, last_band)\n    try:\n        band_file.write_text(json.dumps({\"band\": band}))\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n    if msg:\n        print(json.dumps({\"systemMessage\": msg}))\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    try:\n        main()\n    except Exception:\n        pass  # a warning hook must never break the session\n    sys.exit(0)\n","numLines":143,"startLine":1,"totalLines":143}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"28f3e67e-e7ae-475d-97a8-91ec6cf41881","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"mode"},"session_id":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/pabloportilloolivo/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","version":"2.1.311","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"0747d752-21f4-4f6b-ba9a-e93972ad9aec","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-5-5","id":"msg_011CgS711rjTwQbcuq2SbAGb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"`ctx_guard.py` is a Stop hook that warns you as the context window fills up. One function in it is still a stub.\n\n**What it does:**\n- It runs after every Claude turn and reads the context percentage that `ctx_statusline.py` saved to `.claude/state/ctx_<session>.json`.\n- It prints a `systemMessage` that you see and Claude doesn't. It never blocks and never runs `/compact`.\n- The bands are 50% (plan a compact), 60% (compact at the next green test) and 80% (compact or `/handoff` now). `CTX_WARN_PCT` and `CTX_COMPACT_PCT` override the first two, and the third is always the second plus 20.\n- `log_usage` appends a row per turn to `notes/usage-ledger.csv`, labelled with the contents of `.claude/state/phase`. Your `CLAUDE.md` rule about writing the phase feeds this ledger.\n\n**The stub:** `decide()` at `.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py:61` just raises `NotImplementedError(\"MP2 Exercise A: implement me with /tdd\")`. That is the exercise.\n\n- **What still works:** the ledger logging runs before `decide()`, so rows are still written.\n- **What doesn't:** the `except Exception: pass` at the bottom swallows the error, so no warnings ever appear and nothing tells you why.\n- **What it should do:** the docstring spells out the behaviour. Warn once per upward crossing. When usage drops, store the lower band so the next crossing warns again.\n- **Likely tests:** `band_for`, `message_for` and the `(message or None, band)` return are already there, so the tests are probably something like 40% after NONE gives no message, 55% after NONE gives WARN, 55% after WARN gives no message, and 30% after URGENT gives no message but stores NONE.\n\nI haven't changed anything. If you want to do this exercise, `/tdd` is the intended way, and it's one ticket's worth of work."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2281,"cache_read_input_tokens":63361,"output_tokens":729,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":66},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2281,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":729,"cache_read_input_tokens":63361,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2281,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2281},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgS711c74Dyw7LJZWeo9R","requestRef":"5b559356-6411-402e-8fdf-8e8b60e11100","requestedModel":"claude-sonnet-5-5","type":"assistant","uuid":"c869fc92-6963-4f32-b216-99519ac20298","timestamp":"2026-10-27T04:23:31.728Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/pabloportilloolivo/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"12c0d988-848f-41b0-bf04-f0e482e0340f","version":"2.1.311","gitBranch":"main"}
